Like I said, it's just this heavy background feeling that's always there, even when I'm smiling and doing okay. I try really hard not to let it get to me, though. \n\nAs for how long... um, I guess it's been about two years and four months now? It started right around the time I had to stop taking my community college classes. The money just wasn't working out, and with my schedule as an inspector at the plant, it was just too much to juggle. I mean, I know a lot of people have to work and can't afford school, so I shouldn't really complain about it. I'm lucky to have a job at all, right? But... yeah, I think that's when this constant low feeling started. Does that make sense? I mean, I know working at the plant is tiring for everyone, so I try not to make a big deal out of it. But... it feels like even the small stuff just drains me? \n\nLike, just filling out the inspection logs at work, or driving to play basketball, or even just... washing my hair. It all feels like it takes more energy than I actually have. And even when I try to rest on my days off, I don't really feel... refilled, I guess? \n\nI'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be complaining when I'm just sitting around doing normal things. I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. Is it normal to feel that tired from just everyday stuff?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough, or that you're letting people down, even when there's no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't really think I feel that way? I mean, I try really hard to do a good job at the plant and be there for my friends, so I don't think I'm letting anyone down. I always make sure to pull my own weight. \n\nI know I'm not perfect or anything, but I don't really walk around feeling like I'm not good enough. I just try to do my best with what I have, you know? I hope it's okay that my answer is no for that one. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on, and I definitely don't want to complain about things that aren't actually happening. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, when your mood was not low at all—where you felt genuinely okay or even good—for a day or more?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly, no. I don't think I've had any days where I felt totally fine, not for a long time. I mean, I have moments where I'm smiling or laughing with my friends when we play basketball, and I try to focus on those good parts. I really do try to be positive and not bring people down. \n\nBut as far as having a whole day where that heavy background feeling is just... gone? No, I don't think so. It's always kind of sitting there underneath everything else. I hope that's an okay answer. I don't want to sound like I'm just being dramatic or not trying hard enough to be happy. Is it normal for it to just stay like that all the time?\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if your mood ever changes at all in response to positive events, like seeing friends or being praised at work, even a little bit, or does it just stay the same most of the time?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure? I mean... I definitely smile and I always say thank you when my supervisor says I did a good job on the inspection lines, or when I'm hanging out with my friends. I really try to show that I appreciate it, because I don't want them to think I'm ungrateful or anything. \n\nBut... inside, I don't think the actual feeling really shifts. Like I said, it's just this heavy background feeling that's always there, even when I'm smiling and doing okay. I try really hard not to let it get to me, though. \n\nAs for how long... um, I guess it's been about two years and four months now? The money just wasn't working out, and with my schedule as an inspector at the plant, it was just too much to juggle. I mean, I know a lot of people have to work and can't afford school, so I shouldn't really complain about it. I'm lucky to have a job at all, right? But... yeah, I think that's when this constant low feeling started. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired or that you have less energy than usual, even when you haven’t been doing anything particularly strenuous?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I have been pretty tired lately. I mean, I know working at the plant is tiring for everyone, so I try not to make a big deal out of it. But... it feels like even the small stuff just drains me? \n\nLike, just filling out the inspection logs at work, or driving to play basketball, or even just... washing my hair. It all feels like it takes more energy than I actually have. And even when I try to rest on my days off, I don't really feel... refilled, I guess? \n\nI'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be complaining when I'm just sitting around doing normal things. I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. Is it normal to feel that tired from just everyday stuff?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough, or that you're letting people down, even when there's no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't really think I feel that way? I haven't really had any breaks where I felt like my old self again, even for a few weeks. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be handling things better, and I really am trying to push through it. I hope it's okay that it's been going on for that long without me fixing it. Is that the kind of timeline you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you've noticed that you don't enjoy things as much as you used to, even things you used to really like, like your hobbies or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, I think we might have just talked about this a little bit? I'm so sorry, I hope I didn't answer it wrong the first time. \n\nLike I was saying before with the basketball and everything... I really do still try to show up and do all my hobbies, because I don't want to let anybody down or be a burden. It's just that the actual fun part of it feels like it's gone right now. \n\nDid... did you need me to explain it differently, or did you mean something else? I'm really sorry if I didn't give you enough detail before. I can try to think of a better way to describe it if that's what you need? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there are any activities or hobbies that you used to really enjoy but don't enjoy as much anymore?\n\nPatient: Um... I feel like I'm repeating myself, and I'm really sorry if I'm not giving you the answers you're looking for. I really want to be helpful. \n\nI don't think there's anything specific that I've stopped enjoying completely, or like... a specific hobby that stands out. It's more just that general feeling I was trying to describe before, where everything just feels a little flat, even though I'm still trying my hardest to do them all. \n\nI still go to basketball, I still try to read, I still hang out with my friends... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I mean, I really try to let myself feel happy when good things happen. Like, if I'm with my friends and they're joking around, I definitely laugh and try to be part of it. I don't want to bring the mood down or make them feel like I'm not appreciating them. 

But... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't sound ungrateful, it kind of feels like I'm just going through the motions? Like, I smile and I laugh, but inside, that heavy, flat feeling doesn't really go away. It just sort of stays there underneath everything. 

I'm sorry, I know I should be able to just enjoy the good moments like a normal person. Am I explaining that right? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really do try to be positive.
